    def test_format_files_for_input_minimal_unique_ids():
        # Setup InputOutput instance
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        # Prepare test data with files having same names in different dirs and some read-only
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/module1/file.py",
            "src/module2/file.py",
            "src/module1/utils.py",
            "docs/file.py",
            "README.md",
        ]
        rel_read_only_fnames = [
            "src/module2/file.py",
            "README.md",
        ]
    
        # Call the method under test
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input(rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames)
    
        # The output should contain minimal unique file ids with disambiguation in parentheses
        # and read-only files marked with " R " prefix, editable with "   " prefix.
        # The order is read-only files first, then editable files.
    
        # Expected lines (order matters)
        # read-only files:
        # " R file.py (src/module2)"
        # " R README.md"
        # editable files:
        # "   file.py (src/module1)"
        # "   file.py (docs)"
        # "   utils.py"
    
        lines = output.strip().split("\n")
    
        # Check read-only files first
>       assert lines[0].startswith(" R ")
E       AssertionError: assert False
E        +  where False = <built-in method startswith of str object at 0x7f6931367eb0>(' R ')
E        +    where <built-in method startswith of str object at 0x7f6931367eb0> = 'R file.py (src/module2)'.startswith

___________________ test_format_files_for_input_empty_lists ____________________

    def test_format_files_for_input_empty_lists():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        # Empty lists should return just a newline
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input([], [])
        assert output == "\n"
    
        # Non-empty rel_fnames but empty read-only list
        rel_fnames = ["file1.py", "dir/file2.py"]
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input(rel_fnames, [])
        lines = output.strip().split("\n")
        # All lines should start with "   "
>       assert all(line.startswith("   ") for line in lines)
E       assert False

    def test_format_files_for_input_mixed_and_duplicates():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",
        ]
        rel_read_only_fnames = [
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
        ]
    
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input(rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames)
        lines = output.strip().split("\n")
    
        # Check that read-only files come first and are correctly formatted
        read_only_lines = lines[:2]
        read_only_expected = set([
            " R file.txt (src/b)",
            " R file2.txt (src/a)",
        ])
>       assert set(read_only_lines) == read_only_expected
E       AssertionError: assert {' R file2.tx...file.txt (b)'} == {' R file.txt....txt (src/a)'}
E         
E         Extra items in the left set:
E         ' R file2.txt (a)'
E         'R file.txt (b)'
E         Extra items in the right set:
E         ' R file.txt (src/b)'
E         ' R file2.txt (src/a)'
E         Use -v to get more diff

___________________ test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness ___________________

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "a/b/c/file.txt",
            "a/b/d/file.txt",
            "a/b/c/other.txt",
            "a/b/c/file2.txt",
            "a/b/file.txt",
        ]
    
        minimal_ids = io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
        # minimal_ids should be a dict mapping each full path to a minimal unique id string
        assert isinstance(minimal_ids, dict)
        assert set(minimal_ids.keys()) == set(rel_fnames)
    
        # Check that files with same name have disambiguation
        assert minimal_ids["a/b/c/file.txt"] != "file.txt"
        assert minimal_ids["a/b/d/file.txt"] != "file.txt"
>       assert minimal_ids["a/b/file.txt"] != "file.txt"
E       AssertionError: assert 'file.txt' != 'file.txt'

    def test_format_files_for_input_mixed_and_duplicates():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",
        ]
        rel_read_only_fnames = [
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
        ]
    
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input(rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames)
        lines = output.strip().split("\n")
    
        # Check that read-only files come first and are correctly formatted
        read_only_lines = lines[:2]
        read_only_expected = set([
            " R file.txt (src/b)",
            " R file2.txt (src/a)",
        ])
        # Normalize lines by removing extra spaces for comparison
        normalized_read_only = set(line.strip() for line in read_only_lines)
>       assert normalized_read_only == read_only_expected
E       AssertionError: assert {'R file.txt ...ile2.txt (a)'} == {' R file.txt....txt (src/a)'}
E         
E         Extra items in the left set:
E         'R file2.txt (a)'
E         'R file.txt (b)'
E         Extra items in the right set:
E         ' R file.txt (src/b)'
E         ' R file2.txt (src/a)'
E         Use -v to get more diff

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "a/b/c/file.txt",
            "a/b/d/file.txt",
            "a/b/c/other.txt",
            "a/b/c/file2.txt",
            "a/b/file.txt",
        ]
    
        minimal_ids = io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
        # All keys must be present
        assert set(minimal_ids.keys()) == set(rel_fnames)
    
        # file.txt appears multiple times, minimal ids should disambiguate
        file_txt_ids = [minimal_ids[f] for f in rel_fnames if f.endswith("file.txt")]
        # They should be unique
        assert len(set(file_txt_ids)) == len(file_txt_ids)

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()

            "a/b/d/file.txt",
            "a/b/c/other.txt",
            "a/b/c/file2.txt",
            "a/b/file.txt",
        ]
    
        minimal_ids = io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
        # All keys must be present
        assert set(minimal_ids.keys()) == set(rel_fnames)
    
        # file.txt appears multiple times, minimal ids should disambiguate

        # Patch methods
        def compute_minimal_fileids(self, rel_fnames):
            return {f: f for f in rel_fnames}
    
        def format_files_for_input(self, rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames):
            minimal_unique_fileids = self.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
            def format_minimal_fileid(fname):
                pth = Path(minimal_unique_fileids[fname])
                if len(pth.parts) > 1:
                    return f"{pth.name} ({'/'.join(pth.parts[:-1])})"
                else:

tests/agentsmith_fail2pass_1675.py:246: AssertionError
_______________ test_format_files_for_input_mixed_and_duplicates _______________

    def test_format_files_for_input_mixed_and_duplicates():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        # Patch methods
        def compute_minimal_fileids(self, rel_fnames):
            grouped_fnames = defaultdict(list)
            for full_path in rel_fnames:
                pth = Path(full_path)
                fname = pth.name
                grouped_fnames[fname].append(list(pth.parts))
    
            shared_prefixes = {}
            for fname, paths in grouped_fnames.items():
                shared_prefix = []
                while all(len(path) > 1 for path in paths):
                    next_part = paths[0][0]
                    if not all(path[0] == next_part for path in paths):
                        break
                    shared_prefix.append(next_part)
                    paths = [path[1:] for path in paths]
                shared_prefixes[fname] = Path(*shared_prefix)
    
            minimal_unique_ids = {}
            for full_path in rel_fnames:
                pth = Path(full_path)
                fname = pth.name
                prefix = shared_prefixes[fname]
                minimal_unique_ids[full_path] = str(pth.relative_to(prefix))
    
            return minimal_unique_ids
    
        def format_files_for_input(self, rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames):
            minimal_unique_fileids = self.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
            def format_minimal_fileid(fname):
                pth = Path(minimal_unique_fileids[fname])
                if len(pth.parts) > 1:
                    return f"{pth.name} ({'/'.join(pth.parts[:-1])})"
                else:
                    return pth.name
    
            read_only_files = []
            for full_path in (rel_read_only_fnames or []):
                name = format_minimal_fileid(full_path)
                read_only_files.append(f" R {name}")
    
            editable_files = []
            for full_path in rel_fnames:
                if full_path in rel_read_only_fnames:
                    continue
                name = format_minimal_fileid(full_path)
                editable_files.append(f"   {name}")
    
            return "\n".join(read_only_files + editable_files) + '\n'
    
        io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids = compute_minimal_fileids.__get__(io_instance)
        io_instance.format_files_for_input = format_files_for_input.__get__(io_instance)
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",
        ]
        rel_read_only_fnames = [
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
        ]
    
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input(rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames)
        lines = output.strip().split("\n")
    
        # Check that read-only files come first and are correctly formatted
        read_only_lines = lines[:len(rel_read_only_fnames)]
        editable_lines = lines[len(rel_read_only_fnames):]
    
        # Read-only lines start with " R "
>       assert all(line.startswith(" R ") for line in read_only_lines)
E       assert False
E        +  where False = all(<generator object test_format_files_for_input_mixed_and_duplicates.<locals>.<genexpr> at 0x7f7fee5d9700>)

            minimal_unique_ids = {}
            for full_path in rel_fnames:
                pth = Path(full_path)
                fname = pth.name
                prefix = shared_prefixes[fname]
                minimal_unique_ids[full_path] = str(pth.relative_to(prefix))
    
            return minimal_unique_ids
    
        def format_files_for_input(self, rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames):
            minimal_unique_fileids = self.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
            def format_minimal_fileid(fname):
                pth = Path(minimal_unique_fileids[fname])
                if len(pth.parts) > 1:
                    return f"{pth.name} ({'/'.join(pth.parts[:-1])})"
                else:
                    return pth.name
    
            read_only_files = []
            for full_path in (rel_read_only_fnames or []):
                name = format_minimal_fileid(full_path)
                read_only_files.append(f" R {name}")
    
            editable_files = []
            for full_path in rel_fnames:
                if full_path in rel_read_only_fnames:
                    continue
                name = format_minimal_fileid(full_path)
                editable_files.append(f"   {name}")
    
            return "\n".join(read_only_files + editable_files) + '\n'
    
        io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids = compute_minimal_fileids.__get__(io_instance)
        io_instance.format_files_for_input = format_files_for_input.__get__(io_instance)
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",
        ]
        rel_read_only_fnames = [
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
        ]
    
        output = io_instance.format_files_for_input(rel_fnames, rel_read_only_fnames)
        lines = output.strip().split("\n")
    
        # Check that read-only files come first and are correctly formatted
        read_only_lines = lines[:len(rel_read_only_fnames)]
        editable_lines = lines[len(rel_read_only_fnames):]
    
        # Read-only lines start with " R "
>       assert all(line.startswith(" R ") for line in read_only_lines)
E       assert False
E        +  where False = all(<generator object test_format_files_for_input_mixed_and_duplicates.<locals>.<genexpr> at 0x7f9a842858a0>)

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",

        ]

        # Files with same name get disambiguated by path prefix

        assert minimal_ids["src/b/file.txt"] == "b/file.txt"

    def test_format_files_for_input_handles_relative_and_absolute_paths(tmp_path):

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",
            "file3.txt",
        ]
    
        minimal_ids = io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
        # Files with same name get disambiguated by path prefix
        assert minimal_ids["src/a/file.txt"] == "a/file.txt"
        assert minimal_ids["src/b/file.txt"] == "b/file.txt"
    
        assert minimal_ids["src/a/file2.txt"] == "a/file2.txt"
        assert minimal_ids["src/b/file2.txt"] == "b/file2.txt"

    def test_format_files_for_input_handles_relative_and_absolute_paths(tmp_path):

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():
        io_instance = io.InputOutput()
    
        rel_fnames = [
            "src/a/file.txt",
            "src/b/file.txt",
            "src/a/file2.txt",
            "src/b/file2.txt",
            "src/a/file3.txt",
            "file3.txt",
        ]
    
        minimal_ids = io_instance.compute_minimal_fileids(rel_fnames)
    
        # Files with same name get disambiguated by path prefix
        assert minimal_ids["src/a/file.txt"] == "a/file.txt"
        assert minimal_ids["src/b/file.txt"] == "b/file.txt"
    
        assert minimal_ids["src/a/file2.txt"] == "a/file2.txt"
        assert minimal_ids["src/b/file2.txt"] == "b/file2.txt"
    
        # Files with unique names should just be their name
        assert minimal_ids["file3.txt"] == "file3.txt"

    def test_compute_minimal_fileids_correctness():

        # Files with same name get disambiguated by path prefix

    def test_format_files_for_input_handles_relative_and_absolute_paths(tmp_path):
